Proposition~\ref{prop:best_approx} with $c_L=C_\ell$, $C_L=C_u$.
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- cor-15: body_md -->
\label{cor:red}
With $(\psi^*,q^*,\mathbf{h}^*)$ from Corollary~\ref{cor:hdiv23},
\[
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}-(\operatorname{\mathbf{curl}}\psi+\mathcal{R}_2 q+\mathbf{h})\|_{H(\operatorname{div})}
\le |\psi^*-\psi|_1+\sqrt{1+C_{x_0}^2}\,\|q^*-q\|_0+\|\mathbf{h}^*-\mathbf{h}\|_0 .
\]
Since the full harmonic space ${\mathcal{H}}$ is in the class, $\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}^*$ is admissible and the last term contributes no approximation error,
only finitely many coefficients. Since $q\in{\mathcal{Q}}$ is piecewise, jumps of $q^*$ across coefficient
interfaces carry no approximation penalty beyond the separate subdomain $L^2$ errors. With $(\psi^*,{\bf z}^*)$ from Proposition~\ref{prop:hdiv_regular} with $n=2$,
\[
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}-(\operatorname{\mathbf{curl}}\psi+{\bf z})\|_{H(\operatorname{div})}\le|\psi^*-\psi|_1+\sqrt2\,\|{\bf z}^*-{\bf z}\|_1 ,
\]
and jumps of $q^*$ are carried by kinks of ${\bf z}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:div_kink}).
<!-- end cor-15 -->
<!-- rem-15: body_md -->
\label{rem:keepq}
In the first class the divergence $q$ is an
independent $L^2$ function in the representation of the flux and is
approximated directly: its target $q^*=f_1-{\bf b}\cdot\operatorname{grad} u-cu$ may jump across
coefficient interfaces, and the piecewise classes ${\mathcal{Q}}$ impose no continuity
there. The two residuals in \eqref{loss} stay separate, so
the equilibrium residual $\|q+{\bf b}\cdot\operatorname{grad} v+cv-f_1\|_0$ is available as its
own indicator, and no cancellation between $\mathcal{R}_2 f_1$
and $\mathcal{R}_2({\bf b}\cdot\operatorname{grad} v+cv)$ occurs. Eliminating $q$, as in
the balanced formulation, removes one network and one residual, at
the price of that cancellation and of tying the flux
error to $\|u-v\|_1$. In the second class the equilibrium
residual $\|\operatorname{div}{\bf z}+{\bf b}\cdot\operatorname{grad} v+cv-f_1\|_0$ stays separate in the same way.
<!-- end rem-15 -->
<!-- rem-16: body_md -->
\label{rem:builtin}
The construction is an instance of one rule. Write an equation of the system as $Dz_2+Bz_1=F$, where $D$ has a bounded right inverse $R_D$ ($DR_D=I$) and $B$ acts on the other unknowns, and parameterize $z_2=z_{2,0}+R_D(F-Bz_1)$ with $z_{2,0}$ in a conforming class for $\ker D$. The equation then holds for every parameter value, the functional is the full least-squares functional restricted to $\{Dz_2+Bz_1=F\}$, and its equivalence with the error is inherited (Remark~\ref{rem:variants}). Here $D=\operatorname{div}$, $R_D=\mathcal{R}_2$, $z_2=\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}$, $Bv={\bf b}\cdot\operatorname{grad} v+cv$, $F=f_1$ and $\ker\operatorname{div}=\operatorname{\mathbf{curl}} H^1\oplus{\mathcal{H}}$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:hdiv23}), which gives $\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}_0+\mathcal{R}_2(f_1-{\bf b}\cdot\operatorname{grad} v-cv)$.
<!-- end rem-16 -->
<!-- thm-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:balanced_equiv}
Let $(v,\psi,\mathbf{h})$ satisfy the exact boundary conditions $v=g_D$ on $\partial\Omega$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{balanced_equiv}
 C_\ell\big(\|u-v\|_1^2+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}[v,\psi,\mathbf{h}]\|_{H(\operatorname{div})}^2\big)
 \le {\mathtt{LS}}_{\rm bal}(v,\psi,\mathbf{h};F)
 \le C_u\big(\|u-v\|_1^2+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}[v,\psi,\mathbf{h}]\|_{H(\operatorname{div})}^2\big).
\end{equation}
<!-- end thm-4 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
For the error pair $(u-v,\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}[v,\psi,\mathbf{h}])$ the second residual in \eqref{LS} vanishes by
\eqref{balance_identity}. Hence its full least-squares norm is exactly \eqref{LS_bal}; apply Lemma~\ref{lem:equiv}.
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- cor-16: body_md -->
\label{cor:balanced_approx}
Let $q^*=f_1-{\bf b}\cdot\operatorname{grad} u-cu$ and $\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}=\operatorname{\mathbf{curl}}\psi^*+\mathcal{R}_2 q^*+\mathbf{h}^*$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{balanced_approx}
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}[v,\psi,\mathbf{h}]\|_{H(\operatorname{div})}
\le |\psi^*-\psi|_1
 +\sqrt{1+C_{x_0}^2}\,(\|{\bf b}\|_\infty+\|c\|_\infty)\|u-v\|_1
 +\|\mathbf{h}^*-\mathbf{h}\|_0 .
\end{equation}
Thus, apart from the finite-dimensional harmonic component, approximation of the $H(\operatorname{div})$ flux is reduced to $H^1$ approximation of the two scalar functions $u$ and $\psi^*$.
<!-- end cor-16 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
Use Corollary~\ref{cor:red} with $q=q[v]$ and
\[
 \|q^*-q[v]\|_0
 \le \|{\bf b}\|_\infty\|\operatorname{grad}(u-v)\|_0+\|c\|_\infty\|u-v\|_0.
\]
<!-- end proof-36 -->
<!-- rem-17: body_md -->
\label{rem:no_emulation}
The fixed interface factors $|d_\Gamma|$, the Poincar\'e operator $\mathcal{R}_2$, and the harmonic basis ${\mathcal{H}}$ are deterministic ingredients given in closed form. They are not themselves emulated by
specially constructed neural networks. The trainable objects are ordinary scalar networks approximating the unknown amplitudes. Thus exact $H(\operatorname{div})$ conformity and the prescribed interface locations do not have to be learned by optimization.
<!-- end rem-17 -->
<!-- head-1: body_md -->
$\Omega=(-1,1)^2$, $\Gamma=\{|x-C|=R\}\cap\Omega$ with $C=(-2,0)$ and $R=2.1$, $\mathbf A=\alpha I$ with $\alpha=1$ for $|x-C|<R$ and
$\alpha=3$ outside, ${\bf b}=\frac12(-x_2,x_1+2)$, divergence free and tangent to the circles about $C$, $\boldsymbol{\beta}=0$ and $c=1$;
$r$ and $\vartheta$ are the distance to $C$ and the polar angle about $C$,
${\bf e}_r$ the radial unit vector and $\chi_+$ the indicator function of $r>R$. With $U=\sin(\pi x_1/2+0.3)\cos(\pi x_2/2)$,
\[
 u=U+\chi_+(r-R)V(\vartheta),\qquad {\bf f}_2=\chi_+\,g(\vartheta)\,\frac Rr\,{\bf e}_r,\qquad \mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}=-\alpha\operatorname{grad} u-{\bf f}_2,
\]
$V(\vartheta)=(\pi^2R/3)\,U(C+R{\bf e}_r(\vartheta))$, $g=-(\alpha_+-\alpha_-)\partial_rU-\alpha_+V$ on $\Gamma$, $f_1=\operatorname{div}\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}+{\bf b}\cdot\operatorname{grad} u+cu$ and $g_D=u$. Since $g(\vartheta)R/r\,{\bf e}_r$ is divergence free for
$r>R$, $\operatorname{div}{\bf f}_2=g\,\delta_\Gamma$: the $H^{-1}$ part of the data is a line source
on $\Gamma$, and ${\bf f}_2\in L^2$ is not in $H(\operatorname{div})$. Then $[\![ u]\!]=0$, $[\![ \partial_ru]\!]=V$, $[\![ \mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot{\bf n}]\!]=0$
and $[\![ \mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot{\bf e}_\vartheta]\!]=-(\alpha_+-\alpha_-)\partial_\vartheta U/R$ on $\Gamma$: $u$ has a kink and the flux a
tangential jump. $V$ is chosen so that $[\![ \alpha\Delta u]\!]=0$, which with ${\bf b}\cdot{\bf n}=0$ on
$\Gamma$ makes $f_1$ and $q^*=\operatorname{div}\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}$ continuous across $\Gamma$, so that the divergence, a continuous network output
$q_\theta$ or the divergence of a continuous field ${\bf z}_\theta$, has no
jump to represent, and no module is needed in either. The classes are generic, $\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}_\theta=\operatorname{\mathbf{curl}}\psi_\theta+\mathcal{R}_2q_\theta$ with
$x_0=(0.2,0.1)$, or $\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}_\theta=\operatorname{\mathbf{curl}}\psi_\theta+{\bf z}_\theta$, and $\psi_\theta$, $q_\theta$ or the two components of ${\bf z}_\theta$, and $v_\theta$ are outputs of
one network ($q_\theta$ a single continuous output, not the piecewise class ${\mathcal{Q}}$ of Corollary~\ref{cor:red}) with two $\tanh$ layers
of width $30$ followed by three ReLU layers of width $20$; at the start the first
ReLU layer is fitted to lines in $\Omega$ and
the deeper ones are the identity (active in all of $\Omega$), so that
the initial kinks are those of the first layer. The boundary term is the $H^{1/2}(\partial\Omega)$ norm,
$\|e\|_{0,\partial\Omega}^2+\int_{\partial\Omega}\int_{\partial\Omega}|e(s)-e(t)|^2|s-t|^{-2}\,ds\,dt$, by the product midpoint rule with $256$ points per side,
in which the pairs with equal midpoints are omitted (for a Lipschitz $e$ the
diagonal cell pairs contribute $O(h)$ in total). The training cells are squares of side
$2/64$, refined twice within distance $0.1$ of $\Gamma$ and cut along $\Gamma$,
and the derivative residuals enter by the cellwise moment treatment of Section~\ref{subsec:computation}; training
runs in six phases of fifteen minutes, with refinement and four new units after
each phase. The check ${\mathcal{J}}_{\rm check}$ uses squares of side $2/128$, refined
near $\Gamma$ and cut along $\Gamma$ and all kink sets of the network.
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{cccccccccc}
\toprule
class & width & cells & steps & time & ${\mathcal{J}}_h$ & ${\mathcal{J}}_{\rm check}$ & $|u-u_\theta|_1$ & $\|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}_\theta\|_0$ & $\|\operatorname{div}(\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}_\theta-\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$})\|_0$ \\
\midrule
$\mathcal{R}_2q$ & 40 & 24229 & 422 & 90 min & $6.19\cdot10^{-4}$ & $8.01\cdot10^{-4}$ & $0.28\%$ & $0.26\%$ & $0.11\%$ \\
${\bf z}$ & 40 & 26719 & 274 & 90 min & $1.19\cdot10^{-3}$ & $1.37\cdot10^{-3}$ & $0.30\%$ & $0.27\%$ & $0.27\%$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
At $90$ minutes the two classes give errors of $0.28\%$ and $0.30\%$ in $u$
and of $0.26\%$ and $0.27\%$ in $\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}$; the second run made
$274$ steps, the first $422$. With the first, ${\mathcal{J}}_{\rm check}$ decreased in every phase,
from $4.1\cdot10^{-3}$ after the first to $8.0\cdot10^{-4}$ after the last ($0.60\%$
and $0.26\%$ for $\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}$), and was still decreasing at the time limit. Figure~\ref{fig:ell_generic} shows
the first class. The error of $\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}$ is largest in a
narrow band along $\Gamma$ (Figure~\ref{fig:ell_generic}(a)). No single kink set of the network
lies on $\Gamma$; the tangential jump of $\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}$ is carried by
several kink sets of the first layer close to it (Figure~\ref{fig:ell_generic}(b)), and measured across
$\pm0.005$ it follows the exact jump with a relative error of $7.8\%$ (Figure~\ref{fig:ell_generic}(c)).
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.92\textwidth]{fig_ell_generic.pdf}
\caption{Example~1, generic level, first class: (a) $\log_{10}|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}_\theta-\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}|$; (b) the kink sets of the three ReLU layers and $\Gamma$; (c) the tangential jump of
$\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}$ along $\Gamma$ against the polar angle about $C$: exact, and of $\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}_\theta$ across $\pm0.005$.}\label{fig:ell_generic}
\end{figure}
<!-- end head-1 -->
<!-- head-2: body_md -->
$\Omega$ is the unit ball of $\mathbb{R}^n$, $n=4,8$, $\Gamma=\{x_1=s\}\cap\Omega$ with $s=0.1$, $\mathbf A=\alpha I$ with $\alpha=1$ for $x_1<s$ and $\alpha=3$ for $x_1>s$, ${\bf b}=n^{-1/2}(1,\ldots,1)$, $\boldsymbol{\beta}=0$, $c=1$, so the problem is nonsymmetric. The solution and the flux are chosen independently:
\[
 u=U\ (x_1<s),\quad u=U+(x_1-s)V\ (x_1>s);\qquad \mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}=\mathbf S\ (x_1<s),\quad \mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}=\mathbf S+M{\bf e}_2\ (x_1>s),
\]
$U=n^{-1/2}\sum_r\cos(\pi x_r/2)$, $V=1+\frac12\sin\pi x_2$, $S_r=n^{-1/2}\sin(\pi x_{r+1}/2)+x_1\delta_{r1}$ (indices cyclic), $M=1+2\sin\pi x_3$,
and ${\bf f}_2=-\alpha\operatorname{grad} u-\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}$, $f_1=\operatorname{div}\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}+{\bf b}\cdot\operatorname{grad} u+cu$, $g_D=u$. Then $[\![ u]\!]=0$, $[\![ \partial_1u]\!]=V$,
$[\![ \mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot{\bf e}_1]\!]=0$ and $[\![ \mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}\cdot{\bf e}_2]\!]=M$: the kink of $u$ and the
tangential jump of $\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}$ are independent, and ${\bf f}_2\in L^2$
is not in $H(\operatorname{div})$, since $[\![ {\bf f}_2\cdot{\bf e}_1]\!]=-[\![ \alpha\partial_1u]\!]\ne0$. The classes are
$\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}_\theta=\operatorname{Div}A_\theta+\mathcal{R}_nq_\theta$ with center $0$, or $\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}_\theta=\operatorname{Div}A_\theta+{\bf z}_\theta$ with ${\bf z}_\theta$ an output of
$n$ components without module, since $\operatorname{div}\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}=1$ on both sides; $A_\theta=A_{0,\theta}+mN_\theta$ skew,
$v_\theta=v_{0,\theta}+mv_{1,\theta}$, all outputs of one network with two
$\tanh$ layers of width $32$; $m=|x_1-s|$ (Level~I) or
$m=\operatorname{ReLU}(g_\eta)$ (Level~II) with $g_\eta(x)=\mathbf a\cdot x-c_0+\mathbf w^T\tanh(Wx+\mathbf b_0)+c_2$, $W$
with $10$ rows, initially the plane $x_1+0.15x_2+0.1x_3=s+0.15$. The integrals
are taken on rays from $0$ along $N$ scrambled
Sobol directions, split at $\Gamma$ (Level~I) or at the zeros
of $g_\eta$ (Level~II), with Gauss rules for the weight
$\rho^{n-1}$, so the functional is integrated directly; the boundary
term is the $L^2$ form of Section~\ref{subsec:weak_dirichlet}, $w_b\|v-g_D\|_{0,\partial\Omega}^2$
with $w_b=100$. Level~I is trained by Levenberg--Marquardt; Level~II
in two stages, L-BFGS on all parameters (the interface fixed
for the first $100$ iterations) until the zeros of $g_\eta$
settle, then Levenberg--Marquardt on the network with the
interface fixed. The check ${\mathcal{J}}_{\rm check}$ uses $4N$ directions.
\begin{center}
\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{cccccccccc}
\toprule
$n$ & level & class & $N$ & iterations & ${\mathcal{J}}$ & ${\mathcal{J}}_{\rm check}$ & $|u-u_\theta|_1$ & $\|\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}_\theta\|_0$ & distance \\
\midrule
4 & I & $\mathcal{R}_nq$ & 1024 & 463 & $8.9\cdot10^{-9}$ & $1.5\cdot10^{-8}$ & $1.7\cdot10^{-5}$ & $4.0\cdot10^{-5}$ & -- \\
4 & I & ${\bf z}$ & 1024 & 543 & $4.5\cdot10^{-9}$ & $8.6\cdot10^{-9}$ & $1.3\cdot10^{-5}$ & $3.6\cdot10^{-5}$ & -- \\
8 & I & $\mathcal{R}_nq$ & 2048 & 99 & $8.4\cdot10^{-6}$ & $1.5\cdot10^{-5}$ & $5.9\cdot10^{-4}$ & $1.0\cdot10^{-3}$ & -- \\
8 & I & ${\bf z}$ & 2048 & 97 & $2.1\cdot10^{-6}$ & $4.8\cdot10^{-6}$ & $4.0\cdot10^{-4}$ & $3.7\cdot10^{-4}$ & -- \\
4 & II & $\mathcal{R}_nq$ & 1024 & 586 & $1.5\cdot10^{-3}$ & $2.7\cdot10^{-3}$ & $1.2\%$ & $1.5\%$ & $3.6\cdot10^{-4}$ \\
4 & II & ${\bf z}$ & 1024 & 875 & $9.7\cdot10^{-4}$ & $1.5\cdot10^{-3}$ & $1.0\%$ & $1.0\%$ & $6.3\cdot10^{-5}$ \\
8 & II & $\mathcal{R}_nq$ & 4096 & 548 & $5.9\cdot10^{-4}$ & $7.1\cdot10^{-4}$ & $0.82\%$ & $0.93\%$ & $1.8\cdot10^{-5}$ \\
8 & II & ${\bf z}$ & 4096 & 398 & $1.4\cdot10^{-3}$ & $1.7\cdot10^{-3}$ & $1.2\%$ & $1.5\%$ & $6.0\cdot10^{-5}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
``Distance'' is the mean of $|x_1^*-s|$ over the section $x_4=\cdots=x_n=0$
of $\Gamma$, where $g_\eta(x_1^*,x_2,x_3,0,\ldots,0)=0$ (for the first class the maximum
is $1.4\cdot10^{-3}$ for $n=4$ and $1.4\cdot10^{-4}$ for $n=8$; Figure~\ref{fig:highd_interface}).
With the known interface the module carries the kink exactly
on $\Gamma$; for the first class the errors are $10^{-5}$
for $n=4$ and about $10^{-3}$ for $n=8$, both runs
ended at their time limits of $15$ and $30$ minutes,
and for $n=8$ an iteration takes $18$ seconds.
For the first class with the learned interface, the first
stage moved the zero set of $g_\eta$ from the
initial tilted plane toward $\Gamma$; the interface was then
fixed, and Levenberg--Marquardt was applied to the remaining
network parameters: for $n=4$, $500$ L-BFGS iterations and $86$ Levenberg--Marquardt steps; for
$n=8$, $486$ L-BFGS iterations, at the limit of $84$
minutes for the first stage, and $62$ Levenberg--Marquardt steps, in
$120$ minutes in all. For the first class
at $n=8$, the learned interface lies within $1.4\cdot10^{-4}$
of $\Gamma$ on the section, and the errors are
below $1\%$. With the same network and time, the second class
gives, with the known interface, errors of $u$ and $\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}$
smaller by factors $1.3$ and $1.1$ for $n=4$
and $1.5$ and $2.7$ for $n=8$. With the learned
interface it gives smaller errors and a smaller mean
distance for $n=4$ ($1.0\%$ against $1.2\%$ and $1.5\%$, $6.3\cdot10^{-5}$ against $3.6\cdot10^{-4}$, maximum
$3.8\cdot10^{-4}$), and larger ones for $n=8$ ($1.2\%$ and $1.5\%$ against
$0.82\%$ and $0.93\%$, $6.0\cdot10^{-5}$ against $1.8\cdot10^{-5}$, maximum $5.6\cdot10^{-4}$), where it made
$398$ iterations against $548$. Figure~\ref{fig:highd_interface} shows the first class.
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig_highd_interface.pdf}
\caption{Example~2, Level~II.  (a) Section $x_3=\cdots=x_n=0$ of the unit ball: $\Gamma=\{x_1=s\}$ (gray), the initial zero set of $g_\eta$ (red)
and the learned zero sets for $n=4$ (blue) and $n=8$ (green). (b), (c) Position of the learned zero set on the section $x_4=\cdots=x_n=0$: $x_1^*-s$ with $g_\eta(x_1^*,x_2,x_3,0,\ldots,0)=0$, for $n=4$ (scale $10^{-3}$) and $n=8$ (scale $10^{-4}$).}
\label{fig:highd_interface}
\end{figure}
<!-- end head-2 -->
<!-- lem-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:curl_identity}
For ${\bf F}\in H_0(\operatorname{curl};\Omega)$ and ${\bf W}\in H(\operatorname{curl};\Omega)$,
\[
 {\mathcal{L}}_3({\bf F},{\bf W};0)=\|\mu^{-1/2}\operatorname{curl}{\bf F}\|_0^2+\|\kappa^{1/2}{\bf F}\|_0^2+\|\mu^{1/2}{\bf W}\|_0^2+\|\kappa^{-1/2}\operatorname{curl}{\bf W}\|_0^2 .
\]
<!-- end lem-9 -->
<!-- proof-37: body_md -->
Expanding, the cross terms are $-2({\bf W},\operatorname{curl}{\bf F})+2(\operatorname{curl}{\bf W},{\bf F})$, and $(\operatorname{curl}{\bf W},{\bf F})=({\bf W},\operatorname{curl}{\bf F})$ for ${\bf F}\in H_0(\operatorname{curl};\Omega)$.
<!-- end proof-37 -->
<!-- rem-18: body_md -->
Neither equation is built in. Building in $\operatorname{curl}{\bf E}_\theta=\mu{\bf H}_\theta$ would
require $\operatorname{div}(\mu{\bf H}_\theta)=0$, which a network ${\bf H}_\theta\in H(\operatorname{curl})$ does not
satisfy automatically; parameterizing $\mu{\bf H}=\operatorname{curl}\mbox{\boldmath$\Psi$}$ instead loses the tangential continuity
of ${\bf H}$ across jumps of $\mu$. Only the conformity is structural.
<!-- end rem-18 -->
<!-- lem-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:tr_norm}
For admissible pairs $(\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$},v)$ let $|||(\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$},v)|||^2:=\|\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}-\boldsymbol{\beta} v\|_0^2+\|\operatorname{div}\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}+\gamma v\|_0^2+w_s\|\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}\cdot{\bf n}\|_{0,\Gamma_-}^2+w_u\|v\|_{0,\Gamma_-}^2$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{tr_identity}
 {\mathcal{J}}_{\rm tr}(\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$},v)=|||(\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$},v-u)|||^2 ,
\end{equation}
$|||(\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$},v)|||=0$ only for $(\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$},v)=0$, and $|||(\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$},v)|||\le C\big(\|\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}\|_{H(\operatorname{div})}+\|v\|_0+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}\cdot{\bf n}\|_{0,\Gamma_-}+\|v\|_{0,\Gamma_-}\big)$.
<!-- end lem-10 -->
<!-- proof-38: body_md -->
The problem is linear and $(\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$},u)$ makes every term vanish, which gives \eqref{tr_identity}. If $|||(\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$},v)|||=0$, then $\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}=\boldsymbol{\beta} v$, $\operatorname{div}(\boldsymbol{\beta} v)+\gamma
v=0$ and $(\boldsymbol{\beta}\cdot{\bf n})v=\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}\cdot{\bf n}=0$ on $\Gamma_-$, so $v=0$ there and $v=0$, $\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}=0$ by uniqueness \cite[Lemma~3.1]{LZ:18}. The upper bound is the triangle inequality.
<!-- end proof-38 -->
<!-- prop-11: body_md -->
\label{prop:cl_consistency}
Let $F$ be Lipschitz, or, for a locally Lipschitz flux such as Burgers' flux $F(u)=u^2/2$, let $\sup_m\|v_m\|_{L^\infty(Q)}+\|v\|_{L^\infty(Q)}<\infty$, and let $(\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}_m,v_m)$ with
$\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}_m\in H(\operatorname{div}_{t,x};Q)$, $v_m\in L^2(Q)$ satisfy ${\mathcal{J}}_{\rm cl}(\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}_m,v_m)\to0$, $\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}_m\to\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}$ and $v_m\to v$ in $L^2(Q)$. Then $\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}={\bf f}(v)$ and $\operatorname{div}_{t,x}\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}=0$ in $Q$, and $v$ is a weak solution
of \eqref{conslaw} with the inflow data: $({\bf f}(v),\operatorname{grad}_{t,x}\phi)_Q=\langle g,\phi\rangle_{\Gamma_{\rm in}}$ for every smooth $\phi$ with $\phi=0$ near $\partial Q\setminus\Gamma_{\rm in}$.
<!-- end prop-11 -->
<!-- proof-39: body_md -->
$\|\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}_m-{\bf f}(v_m)\|_{0,Q}\to0$ and $\|{\bf f}(v_m)-{\bf f}(v)\|_{0,Q}\le(1+\mathrm{Lip}(F))\|v_m-v\|_{0,Q}\to0$, with $\mathrm{Lip}(F)$ the Lipschitz constant of $F$ on an interval containing the ranges of the $v_m$
and of $v$, give $\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}={\bf f}(v)$. For $\phi$ as stated, the Green formula in $H(\operatorname{div}_{t,x};Q)$
gives $(\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}_m,\operatorname{grad}_{t,x}\phi)_Q=-(\operatorname{div}_{t,x}\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}_m,\phi)_Q+\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}_m\cdot{\bf n},\phi\rangle_{\Gamma_{\rm in}}$; the first term on the right tends to $0$, and the second to $\langle g,\phi\rangle_{\Gamma_{\rm in}}$ because $\|\mbox{\boldmath$\tau$}_m\cdot{\bf n}-g\|_{0,\Gamma_{\rm in}}\to0$.
<!-- end proof-39 -->
